# Claim-to-Citation Map

High-precision policy: each claim entry is anchored to a single paper's abstract/title metadata. Cross-paper synthesis is limited to clustering similar claim types; do not treat cluster membership as a shared conclusion.

## Field Definitions

- Domain consensus: repeated claim pattern across multiple papers; requires separate review before being written as consensus.
- Single-paper original claim: a paper-specific conclusion extracted from its abstract/title metadata.

## Cluster: BCI-EEG decoding

- Candidate papers: 484
- Consensus status: 待人工核验补充

### Integrating Language-Image Prior into EEG Decoding for Cross-Task Zero-Calibration RSVP-BCI (2025)
- Single-paper original claim: Extensive experiments demonstrate that the proposed model achieves superior performance in cross-task zero-calibration RSVP decoding, which promotes the RSVP-BCI system from research to practical application..
- Claim evidence class: direct sentence from BIB title/abstract/keywords
- Direct supporting experiment/data basis: Neural decoding, Encoding model, Visual reconstruction, Speech/language decoding, Representation alignment, Dataset/benchmark; evidence: Integrating Language-Image Prior into EEG Decoding for Cross-Task Zero-Calibration RSVP-BCI.
- Weakness in support: To overcome this limitation, this study aims to enhance the cross-task zero-calibration RSVP decoding performance.
- Likely reviewer risk: dataset and split protocol not identified from metadata; qualitative reconstruction evidence may be insufficient
- Careful writing form: This paper reports that extensive experiments demonstrate that the proposed model achieves superior performance in cross-task zero-calibration RSVP decoding, which promotes the RSVP-BCI system from research to practical application..
- Do not overuse for: claims beyond verified metadata; dataset-specific claims.

### Decoding speech perception from non-invasive brain recordings (2023)
- Single-paper original claim: The results show that our model can identify, from 3 seconds of magneto-encephalography signals, the corresponding speech segment with up to 41% accuracy out of more than 1,000 distinct possibilities on average across participants, and with up to 80% in the best participants—a performance that allows the decoding of words and phrases absent from the training set.
- Claim evidence class: direct sentence from BIB title/abstract/keywords
- Direct supporting experiment/data basis: Neural decoding, Speech/language decoding, Representation alignment, Foundation model/pretraining, Dataset/benchmark; evidence: Decoding speech perception from non-invasive brain recordings.
- Weakness in support: However, extending this approach to natural speech and non-invasive brain recordings remains a major challenge.
- Likely reviewer risk: cross-subject/session generalization must be verified
- Careful writing form: This paper reports that the results show that our model can identify, from 3 seconds of magneto-encephalography signals, the corresponding speech segment with up to 41% accuracy out of more than 1,000 distinct possibilities on average across participants, and with up to 80% in the best participants—a performance that allows the decoding of words and phrases absent from the training set.
- Do not overuse for: claims beyond verified metadata.

### Dynamic modeling of EEG responses to natural speech reveals earlier processing of predictable words (2024)
- Single-paper original claim: We show that expected words are processed faster – evoking lower amplitude N400-like responses with earlier peaks – and that this effect is driven both by the word’s own predictability and the predictability of the immediately preceding word.
- Claim evidence class: direct sentence from BIB title/abstract/keywords
- Direct supporting experiment/data basis: Speech/language decoding; evidence: Dynamic modeling of EEG responses to natural speech reveals earlier processing of predictable words.
- Weakness in support: In other words, the analysis typically assumes that the response has the same shape and timing for every word – and only varies (linearly) in terms of its amplitude.
- Likely reviewer risk: dataset and split protocol not identified from metadata
- Careful writing form: This paper reports that we show that expected words are processed faster – evoking lower amplitude N400-like responses with earlier peaks – and that this effect is driven both by the word’s own predictability and the predictability of the immediately preceding word.
- Do not overuse for: claims beyond verified metadata; dataset-specific claims.

### Du-IN-v2: Unleashing the Power of Vector Quantization for Decoding Cognitive States from Intracranial Neural Signals (2024)

- Likely reviewer risk: dataset and split protocol not identified from metadata
- Careful writing form: This paper reports that our model achieves SOTA performance on both the 61-word classification task and the 49-syllable sequence classification task, surpassing all baselines.
